- PACK HONESTY: the Finance outcomes name the chains' landing leg; notes on Finance (digest flagship + the three incoming legs), Operations (the hand_off leg), Procurement (the ordered leg), Logistics (the delivered leg + three-way match), Clinic (the billed leg) updated to the new truth; sidebar v1.90 (68 node types - no new nodes).

Stage Summary:
- Py8n v1.90.0 on origin/main: the escalation door's quiet rhythm now REACHES someone - the daily digest lands in a real inbox with a subject line worth scanning - and the departments form CHAINS: a deal won on Sales opens onboarding on Operations, a handoff there opens the invoice on Finance; an order placed on Procurement opens the delivery on Logistics, a delivery confirmed there opens the vendor's bill on Finance. THE OVERDUE-ATTENTION VIEW: attention_feed (business_processes.py) + GET
/processes/attention (declared BEFORE /{process_id} so the word is never
eaten as an id) - every OPEN instance past its SLA across ALL machines,
most-overdue first, each row carrying the door's escalation book (count,
last_delivery, last_detail, acked_by, snooze_until), the machine's policy
line, and the journey_leg flag; terminal states skipped even when the clock
ran on; owner-scoped like every read; the clock compared in PYTHON per the
house SQLite naive/aware discipline (the door's pattern - a naive/aware
comparison in a WHERE clause lies); the clock injectable (now=) for tests.
Frontend: an always-on panel on /processes above the grid (rose when
something is past SLA + the count across machines, emerald "all clear"
otherwise), rows with state/ref/title/process/overdue + book chips
(ack / digest xN / escalated xN) + the journey-leg chip + an "open machine"
jump; the feed re-reads on every mutation (refreshAll) and on mount.

ESCALATION-POLICY EDITING: update_escalation_policy + PATCH
/processes/{id}/escalation-policy (PolicyUpdate body: policy + actor) - the
same loud validation the definition carries (validate_escalation_policy:
unknown keys, unknown channels, the two clocks, to-vs-handlers); policy=null
removes it (v85 semantics return: one knock per stint, event-only); the
stored definition is patched on a FRESH dict (the JSON column is never
mutated in place) so journeys survive; business.policy_updated emitted
(source business, target process, payload names the new policy + summary)
and registered in the events contracts. The door reads the policy fresh at
every sweep (policy_from_definition in escalate_stuck), so the new rhythm
rules the NEXT tick - cadence changes AND knock->digest switches - while
the running instances and their episodes are untouched.
  THE V91 DIGEST_GATE FIX (found by the test, real bug): an item switching
knock->digest mid-episode has a book WITHOUT first_at (its knocks kept
last_at) - digest_gate re-anchored pending_since to now on every tick, so
the window NEVER elapsed and the digest never sent. fresh is now about the
DIGEST clock: state_fresh OR no first_at -> the door anchors the book at
the switch (the window starts when the digest rhythm starts; the cap counts
digest appearances - record_digest_book(count=0) preserves a riding ack),
anchor_only marks that the breach was already announced (the knock
episode's business.stuck) so the door does not emit it twice; a knock-era
ack still holds through the switch (the receipt rides the same episode).
Frontend: an Edit/Add policy button on the machine header (indigo) opening
a form - channel select (event-only/email/sms/whatsapp/telegram/discord),
deliver-to, mode select (knock/digest), cadence by mode (min 60s), max
repeats, message template - Save + Remove, errors surfaced loud.

JOURNEY VISIBILITY ON THE SHELF: _catalog_journeys + journeys on
operator_catalog() - per operator, the cross-department legs as
{direction: out|in, from_process, from_operator, on_state, opens,
due_in_seconds}; out = this operator's machine fires the leg, in = another
operator feeds this one; every leg appears exactly twice (out on the
source's card, in on the target's card; 5 chains -> 10 legs). Frontend:
fuchsia chips (out: "won -> Customer onboarding") and violet fed-by chips
(in: "fed by Lead pipeline") on the operator cards, outbound first, capped
at 3 with "+N more", tooltips naming the fire-state, the target and the
leg's own SLA promise.

Task 77 (v92 - the loop closes from the operator's chair)

"proceed with ack/snooze straight from attention rows, a before/after diff
on policy save, and a drawn 3-operator chain view on operator detail."

THE DIFF ON POLICY SAVE: update_escalation_policy now reads the OLD policy
first (policy_from_definition - both sides of the comparison go through the
SAME validator, so the shapes are canonical: mode filled, defaults named,
the unused clock pinned) and the response carries a RECEIPT -
policy_diff: {before, after, changed} - where changed is the exact sorted
key list this save MOVES (_policy_diff_keys: a no-op save diffs EMPTY, a
knock->digest switch names the rhythm AND both clock keys it re-anchored,
a removal names every key the machine gave back, a field left alone never
shows up). business.policy_updated's payload carries the same `changed`
list, so workflows react to WHAT moved, not just that something did. The
process body keeps its v91 shape - the diff rides beside it (additive, the
v91 tests untouched). Frontend: the /processes policy editor is now a
two-step save - Save policy builds the form's policy, normalizes it
through the same canonical lens as the server (normPolicy), diffs it
against the machine's current policy, and RENDERS the before/after rows
(label per key, rose strikethrough old -> emerald new, `new` badge on
added keys, "nothing changed" refused honestly) BEFORE anything lands;
Confirm save (N) is the only button that PATCHes; Remove walks the same
review (every key struck out); after the save the machine header wears
the SERVER's receipt (policySavedNote: "saved - N keys changed: ...",
dismissible).

ACK/SNOOZE STRAIGHT FROM THE ATTENTION ROW: the v91 row already carried
the door's book; now it ANSWERS it - each row grows an Ack button (hidden
once acked) opening the same inline receipt the machine view uses (by +
note + snooze hrs), posted to the SAME endpoint addressed by the ROW's own
ids (POST /processes/{pid}/instances/{iid}/escalations/ack - no detour
through the machine view); the feed re-reads after the ack, so the row
stays on the feed (still open, still past SLA) now wearing the ack chip;
a snoozed chip (sky, tooltip naming snooze_until) joined the chip row and
the digest/escalated chips were made independent of it (ack + snoozed +
escalated xN co-exist honestly).

THE CHAINS DRAWN ON OPERATOR DETAIL: the shelf's chains are now NAMED and
RESOLVED - _CHAINS (revenue / supply / care, each a walk of (source
process, fire state) pairs) resolves against _JOURNEYS once at import
(_resolve_chains: a leg that is not a journey, or a walk that does not
connect, REFUSES AT IMPORT - the two tables can never drift);
_chains_for(slug) returns the chains the operator sits in with the ordered
operator walk (slug/name/icon/color), the resolved legs (fire state, the
process that opens itself, the leg's own due_in_seconds, from_operator +
opens_operator) and `position` - where this operator's node sits (0 = the
chain starts here, last = the terminus); operator_detail carries `chains`,
operator_catalog cards carry the chain slugs beside the v91 legs. NEW
PAGE pages/marketplace/[slug].vue: the operator's detail room - header
(icon/name/tagline/back + install + the v83 receipt panel), the drawn
chain view (SVG per chain: one node per operator left to right, the
operator you are reading LIT in its own color with a YOU ARE HERE marker,
arrows painted fuchsia when the leg is this operator's to fire or
receive - dashed for pure inbound - each labeled with the fire state, the
process that opens itself and the leg's own SLA; below the drawing, one
honest sentence per leg: "your leg - when X lands on Y, Z opens itself"
/ "fed by <operator>"), then the full install plan (pre-wired machines
with states + escalation + journeys, datasets, workflows, agent, rooms,
queues, campaign, dashboard, wiring notes). The marketplace cards grew a
"View operator detail" link. Sidebar v1.92.

## Task 78 (v93 - the boards watch the door)
Date: 2026-09-07 (UTC+8 session) · Request: "auto-refresh the attention feed on business.stuck, chain view on installed systems with live counts, and an SLA digest preview inside the policy editor" - the three next candidates Task 77 named, delivered as v93 on top of v92 (be7cb60).

THE ATTENTION FEED AUTO-REFRESHES ON BUSINESS.STUCK: the board now rides the same owner-scoped live tail every reactive surface rides (WS /events/stream, the streamUrl handshake) - on a system_event of type business.stuck the feed re-reads ITSELF (600ms debounce) without anybody pressing refresh, the open machine's own board (policy, instances, analytics) quietly moves with it (forms untouched, no openProcess reset), the panel wears a live chip (live - refreshes on business.stuck / reconnecting / connecting) with a pulsing Radio icon, and the name of the breach that last triggered the refresh (ref, machine, +overdue) shows under the header; reconnect is exponential (2s doubling to a 15s cap), the socket and timers are cleaned on unmount, and the Refresh button still works for the skeptic. The backend needed NOTHING new - the wire existed since v80 and escalate_stuck already publishes at commit; the test proves the exact frame: a subscriber queue (what the WS handler holds) receives business.stuck carrying ref / process_id / process_name / instance_id / state / overdue_seconds, the wrapped {event: system_event, **ev} shape the browser JSON.parses, and the row the event names is the row the attention feed serves.

THE CHAINS GO LIVE ON THE INSTALLED SYSTEMS: chains_for_system (services/operators.py, beside the chain tables it reads) resolves _RESOLVED_CHAINS against the system's bound kind="process" components BY NAME - the same name-resolution the journeys themselves use at fire time - and counts the REAL rows: per NODE (a bound machine in the walk) the open instances + how many are past their SLA (terminal states skipped, the clock compared in Python per the house SQLite discipline), per LEG in_state (the source's open instances sitting in the fire state - the hand-off armed), fired (the target's OPEN instances this leg opened itself - they carry the journey link in their context) and overdue (the fired ones past the leg's own SLA promise). GET /systems/{id} carries `chains`; the systems page draws the walk as an SVG (one node per machine with its live counts, fuchsia arrows for armed legs, dashed zinc for half-wired, NOT INSTALLED for absent ends) and says it in sentences: "armed - when Lead pipeline lands on won, Customer onboarding opens itself: 0 sitting in won now, 1 case opened by this leg" / "half-wired - Invoice lifecycle is not installed, the hand-off skips honestly until it is". A partial install shows the chain with complete=false naming what it waits on; a system with no machines draws NO chains (an honest absence, never an empty diagram).

THE SLA DIGEST PREVIEW INSIDE THE POLICY EDITOR: escalation_preview (business_processes.py) + POST /processes/{id}/escalation-preview - what the door would do RIGHT NOW under the DRAFT the form is holding, rendered over the machine's LIVE overdue items and NEVER persisted: the draft goes through the SAME validator a save runs (a broken draft refuses loudly here, before it can be saved), then every overdue open instance walks the draft's own gate - mode=digest: the candidates form the bucket and the ONE summary is typeset (digest_subject + render_digest, the exact strings the email adapter would carry) with the window's honesty named (due now, or next_in_seconds); mode=knock: each episode the gate would knock on becomes a rendered message (draft template or the default, knock_subject included) with its attempt number and rotation target; no policy: the v85 semantics (one knock per stint, event-only). The holds are half the truth - acknowledged (with snooze remaining), too_soon (next attempt in ...), episode_complete (the cap), already_escalated_this_stint (the no-policy one-per-stint) all show up as HELD, and the v88 ack holds even on a no-policy machine. Delivery honesty rides along: event-only / no target / no endpoint bound -> "would skip - ..."; endpoint bound -> "would deliver over email -> ops@...". Pure read + render, proven in the test: no book written, no event emitted, no message sent. The editor typesets it on open and re-typesets on every form change (400ms debounce), so the preview doubles as early validation for the diff review that follows.

FRONTEND /processes: the live-tail block (connectLiveTail/scheduleAttentionRefresh/onUnmounted cleanup) + the preview panel (Eye icon, mode chip, policy_line, the N-past-SLA count, the delivery note colored by would_deliver, the digest email card with subject + pre-wrap body + the window line, the per-message rows, the held rows with reason chips and snooze/next-in details, the draft-refused line). FRONTEND /systems: the Journey chains section on the system detail (between health and architecture): per-chain header (name, story, "live end to end" vs "waiting on <missing>"), the SVG walk with live counts per node and per leg, the armed/half-wired/uninstalled sentences. Sidebar v1.93.

TESTS: tests/test_v93_features.py (4 tests) - (1) the attention live tail: the queue receives business.stuck with the payload keys + the wrapped shape + the feed already carrying the row + a stranger's tail hearing nothing; (2) the system chains with live counts: revenue present on a two-machine floor system with leg0 armed (fired 1) and leg1 half-wired, nodes counting open + the injected clock moving the overdue counts, the bare system drawing nothing; (3) the preview: the digest typeset over live items (subject exact), the knock cadence honesty (too_soon with next_in named), the ack holding, the no-policy v85 semantics, the pure-read proof (no book, no events, nothing delivered), the loud 400s; (4) the version pin. Pins v79-v92 -> 1.93.0. Fixed live during the run: chains_for_system queried process NAMES as ids (0 rows everywhere - the fired/in_state counts caught it); the smoke's in_state expectation had to respect the pack's terminal "won" (the lead CLOSES on landing - in_state 0, fired 1 is the honest truth).

AN ESCALATION-HISTORY SPARKLINE PER MACHINE: process_analytics (business_processes.py) gained the injectable `now` (keyword-only, default None - existing callers untouched) and derives `escalation_history` from the log rows it ALREADY loads: 14 day buckets (window_days: 14, oldest first, ISO day keys), per day {escalations: 'escalated' + the machine's own 'escalate' move, acknowledgements: ACK_TRANSITION, digests: DIGEST_TRANSITION, total} - zero-filled so a quiet stretch reads as DATA, never missing; rows older than the window are honestly out (the top-line counters still read the whole log - the window belongs to the sparkline, not the ledger). ## Task 81 (v96 - the human picks the moment, the grid opens, the history deepens)
Date: 2026-09-08 (UTC+8 session) · Request: "proceed with: ack-and-reschedule on attention rows, drill-down from heatmap cells into a machine's day, and deeper chain history (beyond 5 traversals)" - the exact three next candidates Task 80 named, delivered as v96 on top of v95 (82e835c; the session opened on a stale local v90 mirror - fetched + fast-forwarded first, per the standing lesson).

ACK-AND-RESCHEDULE ON ATTENTION ROWS: the v88 receipt learned to name the door's next knock EXPLICITLY - reschedule_in_minutes on the ack (REST body; the v88 snooze_hours untouched) stamps reschedule_at on the receipt and the episode_gate/digest_gate hold reads it (the reschedule stamp outranks a snooze stamp; one clock per receipt - snooze_hours AND a reschedule together refuse loud 400 "name ONE loan"; negatives refuse at the validator and 422 at the API). While the moment holds the door is quiet with reason=acknowledged + reschedule_at + reschedule_remaining_seconds in the held list, the attention row WEARS the evidence (reschedule_at + remaining, the fuchsia "rescheduled · 28m" chip beside the snooze chip), and when the moment passes the door RE-KNOCKS on the episode's own cadence (attempt count continuing, cap intact) - in digest mode a rescheduled take holds the item OUT of the buckets past the window until the moment passes, then it rides the next digest. The event payload + the ACK log row carry the stamp.

FOUND LIVE (the v96 test caught a v89-v95 latent bug): the ack/knock bookkeeping WIPED the digest clock anchors - record_ack/record_episode rebuilt the episode book without first_at/digest_last_at, so an acked digest item whose loan expired was treated as FRESH (first_at None), the window re-anchored on the next tick and the cap count reset (the digest never caught up). record_digest_book also re-stamped first_at on every send, sliding the anchor. All three writers now carry the ORIGINAL anchors forward within the same state stint (record_digest_book parses the stored isoformat STRING before re-use - found by AttributeError on the first run), so a reschedule/snooze loan expiring re-enters the SAME window with the SAME count.

DRILL-DOWN FROM HEATMAP CELLS INTO A MACHINE'S DAY: escalation_day_detail + GET /processes/escalation-history/{process_id}/{day} (declared beside the v95 grid, before /{process_id}) - ONE heatmap cell, opened: every escalation row the door wrote on that machine between the day's midnights (escalate/escalated, escalation_acknowledged, escalation_digest) read off the transition log with the entity each belongs to (instance join: ref/title/state), each row naming at/kind/actor/note/attempt/overdue + the receipt stamps (snooze_until, reschedule_at), counts + chronological order; a quiet day is data (total 0), a badly shaped day refuses loud 400 BEFORE the machine is loaded, an unknown machine hides 404. The /processes grid cells are clickable (hover-scale, cyan ring on the open cell) and the drill panel below the table lists the day row by row with kind chips (amber escalation / emerald ack / violet digest) + the reschedule/snooze stamps inline.

DEEPER CHAIN HISTORY (BEYOND 5 TRAVERSALS): chain_map(history_limit=5) + GET /processes/chains?history_limit=N (clamped 1..50; the or-default no longer swallows 0 - found by the clamp test) - the per-leg traversal window stretches beyond the recent 5 and every leg names history_limit + history_truncated (opened > shown). The operator-detail chain (marketplace/[slug].vue) grew the depth toggle in the history header ("depth 5 · go deeper" -> "depth 50 · deeper", re-fetching the map) and a per-leg "showing the N most recent of M rides" line when the list was cut - a leg that has ridden for months keeps its older rides one click away.

TESTS: tests/test_v96_features.py (4 tests) - the reschedule end to end (knock at 10s, ack-with-reschedule 30m, held with the stamp at 11m, the attention row wearing the evidence, re-knock attempt 2 at 31m, the one-clock refusal + the 422), the digest-bucket hold (digest #1 lands, ack-with-reschedule 90m, past the 60s window at 30m the bucket is EMPTY with the hold naming the stamp, at 95m the item rides the next digest), the drill-down (today's cell: 2 escalations + 1 ack with the reschedule stamp on the receipt row, chronological, the quiet 2020 day, the 400 day + the 404 machine, the v95 grid intact beside it), the chain depth (7 rides: default 5 + truncated, limit=25 all 7, the clamps, the API wire); pins v79-v95 -> 1.96.0. Sidebar v1.96.

## Task 84 (v99 - the digest pattern applied to the file, the plot's own filters ride the export)
Date: 2026-09-08 (UTC+8 session) · Request: "proceed with: Scheduled chain-history CSV email dispatch (applying summary mode to the file), then direct single-chain/single-leg CSV filtering right on the plot." - delivered as v99 on top of v98 (d7be51c).

THE CHAIN REPORT: one schedule per owner (the new ChainReportSchedule model, unique owner_id, create_all picks it up) - every window the SAME per-leg chain history the plots export renders (chain_history_csv, zero drift, the schedule's own history_limit + optional chain filter) and rides the owner's bound EMAIL endpoint as a REAL MIME attachment. The channel layer grew real attachment support: email_build_outbound(config, to, text, subject, attachments) adds named parts (msg.add_attachment, text/csv default) - multipart/mixed only when a part joins, the no-attachment path byte-for-byte what it always was; deliver_outbound passes attachments to the email builder only (any other provider refuses loud: files-by-chat is not a silent text downgrade) and the masked request carries the attachment names. The subject carries the digest's own scan-line pattern: "[py8n] Chain history - N ride(s) across M leg(s)" (the chain named when the report watches one). Every outcome stamps the schedule row honestly - delivered, skipped (no recipient / no endpoint / an EMPTY window: the door will not email an empty spreadsheet, and the skip still CONSUMES the window the way the digest's window elapses when the bucket stays empty) or failed (smtp refused) - and next_due advances on EVERY due attempt, so a broken endpoint cannot become a per-tick retry storm. The due walk rides the escalation sweep (dispatch_due_chain_reports after escalate_stuck: same session, same commit, the same injectable clock the tests replay; the tick response carries the records under chain_report; the APScheduler job logs each outcome) and the manual door POST /processes/chain-report/send-now dispatches NOW whatever the window says - a real send is a real send. business.chain_report_dispatched lands on the event door (registered in the contracts; the honest skip IS the record). API: GET/PUT/DELETE /processes/chain-report + send-now (all declared before /{process_id}; PUT validates loud - a recipient with an @, cadence >= 300s, depth at the API's own 1..50 ceiling (422), one optional chain name; the new cadence re-anchors next_due like the digest's mode switch). The /processes board grows the chain-report card beside the heatmap: scheduled/paused chip, cadence, next due, the last-result receipt (delivery color, rides, filename), the loud form, Send now / Edit / Remove.

THE PLOT'S OWN FILTERS: GET /processes/chains/history.csv gains chain (one chain, case-insensitive) and leg ("from_name|on_state", case-insensitive) - both compose off the SAME map; an unknown chain or leg is an HONEST EMPTY file (header only, leg_count 0), never a 404 - the map the operator is looking at is the truth. The response echoes the filters it honored (X-Py8n-Chain-Filter / X-Py8n-Leg-Filter) and the filename carries the chain's slug. The plots wear the buttons: per-chain CSV beside every chain name on the operator-detail chain (marketplace/[slug].vue) AND the systems journey chains, per-leg CSV chips on every leg's history line - the file is exactly the slice you are looking at.

## Task 87 (v103 - the branded front door, and the deployment answers for its own liveness)
Date: 2026-09-08 (UTC+8 session) · Request: "proceed" (continuing v102's next candidates: the branded login surface consuming the by-domain door, deployment ping health) - delivered as v103 on top of v102 (32c76b7).

THE LIVENESS PING: POST /systems/{id}/deployment/ping asks "does this system's custom domain actually answer?" - one outbound GET to the DERIVED URL (https://{domain}, the same derivation deployment_out speaks, zero drift), timed, the result stamped on the deployment row (last_ping_at/ok/ms/code/detail - evidence per probe, never a stored "healthy" flag that could silently rot) and written to the operations log (deployment_ping). The probe NEVER raises: a domain that does not answer is an honest answer (ok=false + what happened: ConnectError / timeout / HTTP 5xx), never a 500 - the failure IS the evidence. A deployment with no domain (or no record) refuses loud (400 "no custom domain to ping"). Editors only (it costs an outbound call). The probe target honors PY8N_DEPLOY_PING_OVERRIDE (dev/test only - sandbox domains do not resolve; never set in production). The projection carries the full evidence (deployment_out.last_ping: at/ok/ms/code/detail, null when never asked) and the estate health row wears the compact form (at/ok/ms). Route declared BEFORE /{verb} on purpose - "ping" is its own resource, not a status verb. Models: five nullable columns on system_deployments + the db.py ALTER migration for pre-v103 dev databases.

THE BRANDED FRONT DOOR: pages/go/[domain].vue - the public landing consuming the v102 door. Nothing answers there -> an honest dark page ("Nothing answers here" + the door's own detail + the way back to the platform login); the deployment is live -> the system's OWN face before login: the accent color tinting the whole page (radial gradient from the brand), the logo glyph block, the login headline as the title, the tagline as the subtitle, the system name + environment chips, and a sign-in that lands in the estate (auth.login -> /systems). A visitor who already holds a valid token gets "Continue as <role>" (the door answered my_role; the token is revalidated before walking in, an expired one falls back to the form). The page lives at /go/{domain} - in production host-routing maps a custom hostname onto it; in dev it is the demo path (localhost:3000/go/ops.acme.com). /go/ joined the auth guard's PUBLIC list. useHead titles the tab with the headline.

THE RE-FRAME FINISHED: three stragglers the v102 rewrite missed - login.vue's footer ("Python-native workflow automation"), the sidebar's tagline ("Workflow automation") and the dashboard header ("Python-native workflow automation") all now speak the platform's real name: the Python-native business operations platform / business operations.

FRONTEND: the systems drawer's Deployment panel grew the "Ping now" button (Activity icon, editors, only when a domain is set) and the last-ping readout chip (emerald "answered HTTP 200 · 42ms" / rose "no answer" + the time) beside the live URL; the note line reports the probe's verdict in plain words.

The operations log stays honest WITHOUT flooding: steady-state probes update the evidence columns silently; only TRANSITIONS are loud - deployment_ping_lost (answering -> dark, or the first probe dark) and deployment_ping_recovered, each written to the ops log AND emitted as system.deployment_ping_lost/_recovered on the system's event thread, so a workflow can react to a dark domain. Already-dark stays silent (no per-tick noise). The walk never raises (per-deployment try - the failure IS that row's evidence). Nothing stored: the dot reads the newest evidence, exactly the v103 discipline.

SYSTEM API KEYS (the machine identity): SystemApiKey (system_api_keys, system_id FK CASCADE, sha256 hash + display prefix, scopes, created_by, revoked_at) - py8n_sys_ is a RESERVED prefix auth resolves against this table ONLY (never the v41 user keys). The role is DERIVED from scopes at read time (write -> editor, else viewer) - the same ladder the human members use, topping out at editor: owner doors (members, keys, dissolve) stay human-only. MINT: POST /systems/{id}/keys (owner only) -> the full key shown exactly once; GET /{id}/keys (masked); DELETE /{id}/keys/{key_id} (revoke = stamp revoked_at; the next request carrying it resolves to nothing); every mint/revoke lands in the operations log. THE BOUNDARY IS AIRTIGHT: get_optional_user stamps a resolved system key on request.state.py8n_system_key (+ rides the v43 scopes channel so the router-level read/write gate covers keys with zero new wiring); enforce_auth treats a stamped key as authenticated in enforced mode; and _get_system now takes `request` as a REQUIRED argument (25 call sites swept) - a key on a FOREIGN system is a 404 THERE, never a fall-through to the member lookup, because auth-off's anonymous is an owner and a machine credential must not inherit that. ## Task 89 (v105 - the keys ride the process doors, and the front door gains the system's own work surface)

Date: 2026-09-09 · Version: 1.105.0 · Baseline: 573 passed + 7 skipped -> 577 passed + 7 skipped

THE KEYS RIDE THE PROCESS DOORS (v105): the py8n_sys_ credential can now DO the
system's work, not just describe it. The processes API grew an authority layer
(_door_authority) consulted by every door, decided UPFRONT:

* a SYSTEM key answers only for the machines bound to its system
  (SystemComponent kind=process): advance / ack / start / annotate / reads ride
  the key when the machine is bound; an unbound (or nonexistent) machine looks
  nonexistent (404) - never the anonymous fall-through, because auth-off's
  anonymous is an owner and a machine credential must not inherit that. Writes
  need the write scope (its role tops out at editor; the router-level scope gate
  covers the rest). The key's moves NAME the key: the journey receipt says
  "key:<name>" - provenance a spoofable actor string cannot fake.
* the ESTATE-WIDE views (list, attention, chains, history.csv, chain-report
  CRUD + send-now, escalation-history grid) are the OWNER's cross-machine
  picture: a key gets an honest 403 naming the doors it does get.
* the system's PEOPLE act with the same boundary: a human who is not the
  machine's owner may advance/ack (editor+) or read (viewer+) when they hold
  the role on a system that BINDS the machine - the system's users operate the
  system's machines, the authority the work surface stands on. A member below
  the move's role gets an honest 403; a stranger keeps the old behavior.
* advance_instance grew the instance-process cross-check (optional process_id
  kwarg; the API passes it) every other instance door already kept - authority
  decided on one machine must never move another machine's entity through a
  mismatched path.

THE FRONT DOOR'S WORK SURFACE (v105): GET /systems/by-domain/{domain}/work - the
public domain router's second door, self-gated: the system's own key reads its
work (foreign key 404); the system's people read it with their token (any v62
role); enforced-mode anonymous is told to sign in (401); a signed-in stranger is
honestly refused (403); auth-off's anonymous is the owner (platform convention).
The payload is system_work_surface (business_processes.py): every bound machine
with its live operation (open / stuck, _stuck_state - the same derivation every
view uses) and the attention rows - open instances past their SLA, most-overdue
first, the escalation book per row (count, the ack receipt, the reschedule loan)
- the SAME predicate the estate feed runs, scoped to the system's machines,
terminal states skipped, the clock compared in Python (v38 GOTCHA).
pages/go/[domain].vue stopped being a hallway: sign-in (or "Continue as
<role>") now lands ON the work surface - the totals strip (machines / open /
past SLA / attention), the attention list (ref, machine, state, overdue
humanized, the door's knocks, "taken by X"), and the take form for editor+
(handler, note, snooze hours OR reschedule minutes - one clock per receipt)
riding the machine-board's own ack door. The branded header and the dark page
are untouched; a viewer gets the read-only surface.

TESTS: tests/test_v105_features.py (4 tests) - the keys round (key start +
advance + read on the bound machine, the receipt naming the key, unbound 404,
read-only 403, five estate-wide doors 403, the mismatched-path cross-check, the
key's ack on a knocked instance, the unbound ack 404); the people round (editor
advance + ack through the binding, viewer read / honest 403 write, stranger
keeps the old honesty, membership never reaches unbound machines); the work
surface round (owner token payload + scoping to the system's machines, auth-off
anonymous = owner, the system's key reads its work / a foreign key 404s,
stranger 403, enforced anonymous 401, paused = dark 404, the ack answering
"taken by"); the pin. FOUND LIVE: the double-clock race - the knock rode the
injected clock (base+15s) while the surface reads the REAL one, and the whole
block ran in under a second, so the 1s-SLA instance was not yet overdue to the
surface; the v96 discipline applies (await asyncio.sleep(2.0)). Also re-learned:
the advance door maps ProcessError to 400 (the stranger's move refuses 400, not
404 - the old behavior, now pinned).

Version 1.105.0 (config + 25 test pins re-pinned + sidebar v1.105).
VERIFIED: 577 passed + 7 deliberate skips (573 -> 577, no regressions) + bun run
build green (1.94 MB) + scripts/smoke_v105_live.py 3/3 green on a real uvicorn -
(1) KEYS ON THE DOORS: the key advanced the bound machine over the wire (a ->
b), read it, the unbound machine 404'd, the read-only move 403'd, the
estate-wide views refused the key; (2) THE SYSTEM'S PEOPLE: the invited editor
moved the owner's machine (the binding is the authority), the viewer read and
their move refused honestly; (3) THE WORK SURFACE: the tick door knocked the
overdue instance on the real clock, {domain}/work resolved the system's pending
work (2 machines, 1 attention row with the book), the system's own key took the
turn, the surface answered "taken by erp-job". Smoke sqlite artifacts cleaned.

Stage Summary:
- Py8n v1.105.0: the deployed system is OPERABLE - its key moves its own
  machines' work AS the system, its people advance and take their own
  escalations, and signing in at the company's address lands on the company's
  pending work. The commercial arc (customer.com -> System -> Users -> Business
  operations) now closes inside the front door itself.
- Next candidates: per-domain TLS notes on the route sheet, the work surface

## Task 90 (v106 - advance beside the acks, per-domain TLS notes, the liveness on the surface)

Date: 2026-09-09 · Version: 1.105.0 -> 1.106.0 · Baseline: 577 passed + 7 skipped -> 581 passed + 7 skipped

Session opened on a STALE inherited summary (claimed v98 as HEAD with the v105
round unstarted); fetch showed origin/main at 3cd88a4 (v105) - fast-forwarded,
environment restored (requirements + torch CPU wheel after another sandbox
reset), and the round built exactly along Task 89's named next candidates.

ADVANCE BESIDE THE ACKS (v106): system_work_surface's attention rows now carry
`transitions` - the moves the machine allows FROM the row's current state,
resolved by the SAME _allowed_from the advance door runs, so the surface's
buttons can never offer a move the door would refuse (a state with no outgoing
moves wears an empty list and the surface hides the control). The front door
grows the Advance control beside the take: the move's own form (transitions as
"name -> to" choices, an optional note, one form open at a time - opening one
closes the other) riding the SAME v105 authority the ack rides. Pinned: the
viewer's move refuses 403, the journey receipt names the human (actor), a row
advanced but still late STAYS on the attention list wearing its NEW state's
transitions (the clock never lied), and the terminal move retires the row from
the list entirely.

PER-DOMAIN TLS NOTES ON THE ROUTE SHEET (v106): _tls_note on every live site
block of GET /systems/deployment/routes.caddy - the posture (production leans
on Caddy's automatic HTTPS, staging rehearses on the ACME staging issuer
sparing the production rate limits) followed by what THAT domain's liveness
probe last saw: "certificate live: last probe answered HTTP {code} in {ms} ms
({stamp})" when it answered, "CHECK THE CERTIFICATE: last probe failed
({detail}, {stamp})" when it did not, "no probe evidence yet" when never
asked. The sheet an operator wires at the edge now also answers "is this
certificate doing its job" per domain; regenerated with every deploy verb,
dark deployments still comments-only (v104 contract intact, re-pinned).

THE LIVENESS RIDING THE SURFACE (v106): system_deployment grew liveness() and
_last_ping_out (ONE writer for the evidence shape - the estate row and the
front door can never drift); GET /systems/by-domain/{domain}/work answers
`liveness` {domain, status, environment, last_ping} - the people on a custom
domain see their own address is being watched, an honest null when never
probed, never a lying green. The page wears it as "This address": pulsing
emerald "answered HTTP {code} - {ms} ms - probed N ago" / rose "no answer" +
detail / zinc "never probed - liveness unknown".

ALSO FIXED LIVE: the go/[domain] template's Clock3 icon was never imported
(the v105 build passed with an unresolved component - Nuxt does not fail the
build; it renders as nothing). Imported with the v106 additions.

TESTS: tests/test_v106_features.py (4 tests - the advance round incl. the
viewer 403 + the journey naming + the state-wearing + the terminal retirement;
the TLS notes incl. the stamped-ok block, the REAL refused-probe CHECK block,
the staging never-probed block + dark-stays-comment; the liveness round incl.

read; the pin). FOUND LIVE while writing them: the route-sheet block splitter
